What changed, and why

Ten systematic changes, each broken into the changes that make it up, each of those with the diff of every file it touched. Every dropdown is generated from the worktree by build-diffs.mjs; nothing in a dropdown was typed by hand.

1

The element model and the applier

Before, a slide element was a box of blocks: { frame, blocks: ContentBlock[], overflow, format }. There was no way to say that an element was a shape, a line, a picture or a table, no paint, and no groups; the applier could only reach an element that sat directly on a slide, moved frames by a special case, and could not create a missing intermediate object. Now an element says what it is through a discriminated content and has a paint; a block can override its named style field by field; the applier finds any identified node anywhere in the deck and walks the rest of the path from there.

Why this shape

The reviews asked for shapes with text inscribed, lines with ends, tables with per-cell borders, groups that move as one, per-object font size, fills and borders and shadows. Each is a field on the element or its content, so every lens, the Layers panel and the renderer can discriminate on content.type and nothing has to guess from the blocks it holds.

1.1 · An element says what it is and has a paint

SlideElement gains content: ElementContent — text, shape, line, image, table, chart, group — and paint?: ElementPaint — fill, stroke (the panels call it the border), opacity, corner radius, shadow. overflow becomes optional. locked is added for the Layers panel. Point, ShapeKind, LineEnd and Dash are named. AspectRatio becomes any W:H of two whole numbers rather than two literals. A table's content carries rowHeights, because a slide table has a fixed frame to divide and a document table does not.

1.2 · A block may override its named style, and a style may set vertical alignment

BlockFormat gains fontFamily, fontSize, color, lineHeight, spaceBefore and spaceAfter. This is what makes one shape's text 40px while every other block wearing the same style stays at 20 — the second review found the Size control changing every shape at once because it wrote to the named style. TextStyle gains verticalAlignment so the Style panel can offer top / middle / bottom rows. The comments in both files went, per the repository's rule for any file touched.

1.3 · Layouts carry an id and the op vocabulary can name them

SlideLayout.id is added so a layout is an identified node like everything else (the applier finds nodes by id, and layouts had only a key). DeckTarget gains "layout"; set's target becomes optional because the path-first applier no longer needs it to decide anything.

changedsrc/lib/representation/data/types/slide-decks/op.ts+3 −3 
@@ -1,7 +1,7 @@-type DeckTarget = "slide" | "element" | "section" | "block" | "atom" | "mark";+type DeckTarget = "slide" | "element" | "section" | "layout" | "block" | "atom" | "mark";  export type SlideDeckOp =-  | { op: "set"; target: DeckTarget; path: string; value: unknown; was: unknown }+  | { op: "set"; target?: DeckTarget; path: string; value: unknown; was: unknown }   | {       op: "insert";       target: DeckTarget;@@ -20,7 +20,7 @@ export type SlideDeckOp =     }   | {       op: "move";-      target: "slide" | "element" | "section" | "block";+      target: "slide" | "element" | "section" | "layout" | "block";       path: string;       id: string;       after: string | null;

1.4 · The applier finds anything by id and walks the rest of the path

applyOps is rewritten. A path's first segment is a root field or any id in the deck (findNode / mapNode search the whole tree, skipping two-key { kind, id } objects so a reference to another resource is never mistaken for the node it points at). set walks deep, creating missing objects, deletes on null, and refuses to land on a list of identified items or to walk through a primitive. insert, remove and move act on any identified list. text splices a literal atom and shifts marks. nodeIn is exported for the procedures. The special-cased frame mapping is gone. The tests were rewritten to the new element shape and cover reaching into groups, notes, marks and the refusals.

1.5 · The seed speaks the new shape

Every element in slideDeckSnapshots.json is rewritten as content + paint; text boxes say overflow: "grow"; the first deck gains a shape with text, a line with an arrowhead and a three-by-three table so every lens has something to open on; layouts get ids. commentThreads.json anchors the deck thread to the pink shape (within: { kind: "element", elementId: "el-5" }) with a quote, so the badge, the threads lens and the comment lens are exercised by a fresh seed. The other two threads are only re-indented by the seed writer.

2

The workspace knows the deck's views

A lens or panel is reachable only if its key is declared, so the new lenses needed keys, the rail needed the reviewed order, and a selection needed to carry more than one id. The keys the editor no longer has a file or a rail entry for were removed so that the merge lands on the goal state rather than on a vocabulary that names panels nobody can open: the context keys context, layout-layouts, layout-objects, layout-theme, layouts, notes, overview and stage, and the inspector keys deck, element, layout, locked-element, named-style, placeholder, text-block and theme. slide-deck-editor.deck stays as the category's content view key, which is a different list. Nothing under app-views referred to any of them.

2.1 · New lens keys, two new context keys, sixteen dead keys gone

Added — inspector: cell, chart, group, image, line, next-letter, shape, table, text-box, text-selection, threads; context: prompts, templates. Removed: the sixteen listed above. Both the type union and the runtime list, kept in alphabetical order as the files are. The paragraph in the workspace state's shared.md that described three of the removed keys as “named and unreachable” went with them.

2.4 · A selection may carry several ids

Selection.ids? — a multi-selection is { kind: "elements", id, ids }, a cell selection is { kind: "cells", id: tableId, ids: cellIds }. The workspace state stores and persists it like any selection; only the deck's procedures read it.

3

The runtime

One line. Runtime.apply deep-copies its ops through JSON before buffering them. The views build ops from values read out of the live body, which is a Svelte $state proxy; an op that carried such a value (a frame, a block, a whole element in an insert) was a live reference — it changed under the buffer when the body changed, and the store's serialiser refused proxied structures. Copying at the boundary means the buffer owns plain data and the change set that leaves is exactly what was applied. Everything else the runtime does — buffer, coalesce, flush, rebase, revert, undo — is as it was; page 08 describes it.

changedsrc/lib/model/client/slide-deck-runtimes/definition.svelte.ts+1 −1 
@@ -58,7 +58,7 @@ export class Runtime implements SlideDeckRuntime {   apply(ops: readonly SlideDeckOp[]): void {     if (ops.length === 0) return; -    apply(this, ops);+    apply(this, JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(ops)) as SlideDeckOp[]);     this.schedule();   }
4

The renderer: from Konva to a DOM surface

Before, the content view drew the slide on a Konva canvas, and needed procedures/tokens.ts to resolve --token-* custom properties into literal colours the canvas could paint. Text could not be edited in place, marks could not be rendered per run, nothing was accessible, and the Slides panel drew its own thumbnails. Now there is one authored component, components/authored/slide-surface, that draws a scene with DOM and SVG, edits text with a contenteditable engine, and is used by the centre, the Slides panel and the Style panel's layout previews alike. Tokens resolve through CSS var(), so the resolver is deleted. Nothing imports Konva any more, and the dependency is removed from package.json; pnpm install then dropped it from node_modules and from pnpm-lock.yaml, which git ignores in this repository and which therefore has no diff below.

Why an authored component and not part of the editor

The renderer takes a SurfaceScene — plain data — and callbacks. It knows nothing of bodies, ops or the workspace, which is what lets three views share it and what keeps the lint's view-takes-ids-and-callbacks rule satisfied on the views that mount it.

4.1 · The stage, the overlay and every gesture

One div.stage at the slide's unit size scaled by one CSS transform; items inside it; an overlay above with outlines, handles, guides, the marquee and comment rings. A gesture state machine: press → move, resize by eight handles (shift keeps ratio, alt resizes about the centre), rotate (shift snaps to 15°), line endpoints, marquee (from the stage or from the pasteboard around it, contained selection only), and cell ranges with a live highlight while dragging. Page 07 has the table.

4.2 · One element

Shapes as SVG paths (rectangle, ellipse, triangle, diamond, arrow, callout) with the stroke drawn inside the frame; text plates; pictures; tables as a CSS grid from columnWidths and rowHeights with per-cell fill, rim border and vertical alignment; lines as SVG with markers and a wide transparent hit stroke. Text insets follow the shape so the words stay inside an ellipse or a callout body. A ResizeObserver reports the needed height for a text box whose overflow is grow.

4.3 · The text engine

A contenteditable whose every beforeinput is intercepted and turned into { blockId, from, to, insert } so the body, not the DOM, is the source of truth; the DOM is re-rendered from the runs and the caret is put back where the edit left it. It reports the caret on every selection change, places the caret where the pointer landed on entry, and can shrink text to fit for the shrink overflow.

5

The panel vocabulary

Five authored panel components changed, each for a specific review finding, each keeping its existing callers working. These are shared with the document and spreadsheet editors; the non-picker colour radiogroup and the wrapping choice rows are still there for them.

5.1 · PanelNumber: a plain field, no steppers

The first review: “no +/− on number pickers”. The input-group with two buttons becomes one Input with the unit drawn inside its end padding; ↑/↓ step by step, ⇧ by ten steps; the value commits on Enter or blur. The draft string is initialised in an effect rather than from value at declaration, which is what svelte-check's state_referenced_locally objected to.

5.2 · PanelColor: the native picker, opened directly

The second review: “the colour picker should open the picker directly”. A picker mode draws a 28px swatch wrapping an invisible <input type="color">; on pointerdown or focus the input is primed with the current colour (a hex as-is, a token by reading the swatch's computed background), so the dialog opens on the colour that is there. clearable adds the × that sends ""; a diagonal none-mark shows an empty value. Callers pass no options in picker mode.

5.3 · PanelChoice: icons, a full row, never off

6

The editor's procedures

Before, two files: deck.ts knew slides and one element frame; stage.ts knew two ratios and a metrics panel. Everything else lived in the views. Now each concern is a pure module with tests — and every op the editor ever produces is made by one of these, so the views hold no body logic.

6.1 · deck.ts: the body's shape

Added: placedOn (walks groups and gives every element an absolute frame, depth and parents), relativeTo/within, slideHolding, elementIn, blockIn, holderOf, valueAt, withSet/withSets (a set that changes nothing is no op), withElementFrame (group-aware), withInsertedElements, withoutElements, withRestacked/withRestackedSet/withReorderedElement, withGrouped/withUngrouped, withDuplicatedElements, withSavedLayout/withoutLayout, withNotes, emptyText, freshElement, labelOf. withNewSlide takes a layout key. The type re-exports grew so views never import representation. Removed: framePath, elementBox, slideLengths.

7

The content surface

content/deck.svelte is rewritten around the new renderer. What it keeps from before: the title / canvas / strip grid, the deck title read from the store, the runtime attached in an effect, Previous / Next, the zoom controls and the sync label. What is new: the scene, every callback the surface offers (select, cells, clear, place, frames, rotate, line, grow, enter, exit, edit, caret, badge, context), the window keyboard handler (Escape, Delete, arrows, ⌘G / ⇧⌘G, ⌘D, Enter and type-to-start), the context menu that inserts at the pointer, click-to-place, zoom toward the selection, comment badges from the thread rows, and the rule that the edited holder stays selected while typing. Page 07 is the full account.

8

The context panels

One panel was rewritten, six were written, one was deleted, three placeholders were added. The Stage panel's one useful fact — the ratio — moved into the Style panel as two numbers; the rest of it (design units, drawn size, characters per line) was reporting, not editing, and the second review asked for it to go. Page 05 describes each panel as it is.

8.1 · Slides, rewritten

The number sits above a full-width preview drawn by the shared renderer; only the preview takes the hover and chosen outline; New ▾ offers the deck's layouts; Duplicate and Delete stretch and show their words when the flank is wide enough (a container query); drag to reorder with an insertion bar; ⌥↑ / ⌥↓ step a slide. The measured reel width subtracts the panel's own padding, which is what had been clipping thumbnails on the right.

8.5 · Find and Comments

Find searches text boxes, shape text, table cells and notes (scoped All / Slides / Notes), opens a hit as a text selection on its slide, and can replace one or all through replaced. Comments lists the deck's threads from the store, narrowed to the deck, the slide or the selected object, with a composer that posts through the comments capability.

9

The inspector lenses and their shared sections

Fourteen lens files and seven shared sections, all new. A lens is thin: it resolves what is selected, draws crumbs and actions, and mounts sections; a section takes an id, reads the runtime itself, and writes one path. Page 06 goes through each.

9.1 · Shared sections

SectionShowsWrites
text-styleStyle: named style, font + size, FG / BG, marks. Paragraph (optional): horizontal and vertical alignment. Spacing (optional).<block>/style, <block>/format/*; FG on a range → a coloured mark; BG → the holder's paint/fill, the cell's format/background, or the block's own.
element-geometryX Y W H (fractions) and Turn.<el>/frame via withElementFrame, <el>/rotation.
element-paintFill (optional), border colour + width, dash.<el>/paint/fill, <el>/paint/stroke/*; width 0 or clearing the colour removes the stroke.
element-effectsOpacity, corner (optional), shadow direction (popover of nine) + intensity.<el>/paint/opacity, cornerRadius, shadow via shadowOf.
element-orderPosition n of m, four restack icons.move ops via withRestacked.
arrange-sectionAlign, distribute, match, order for a set (the selection or a group's children).Frames via withElementFrame; withRestackedSet.
comment-action“Comment” or “n comments” for one object.Nothing; inspects the threads lens.

9.2 · Lenses

KeyOpens onSections
slideSlide chosen, canvas cleared, Slides panel clickNotes summary + Edit notes; Hidden; background (theme swatches); Reset to layout. No layout picker — a slide's layout is not changed after the fact.
text-boxA text elementText style (whole block); Height (grow / shrink / clip); Geometry; Fill and border; Effects; Order; Comment.
shapeA shapeKind; Geometry; Fill and border; Effects; Order; Comment. No Text section — click into the shape to style its text.
lineA lineFrom / To / Length / Angle; Ends; Border; Effects (no corner); Order.
imageA pictureSource kind, alt text, URL; Geometry; Effects; Order.
tableA tableRows and columns (Insert / Delete at the end); Geometry; Border; Effects; Order.
cellCells clicked or dragged inside a selected tableMerge / Split; text style (one cell) or alignment (several); Fill and border per cell; W / H as a share of the table; Row / Column × Insert / Delete relative to the first picked cell.
groupA groupUngroup; Members; Geometry; Align / Distribute / Order for the children; Effects.
multi-selectionSeveral objectsGroup / Duplicate / Delete; Selected list; Align / Distribute / Order.
next-letterA collapsed caretText style at the caret.
text-selectionA rangeText style over the range (FG becomes a mark).
speaker-notesNotes button, Find hit in notes, Edit notesThe notes in an editable box on a background of their own; Style only (no paragraph, spacing or counts).
threadsComment action, a badgeComposer; open and resolved threads on that object or slide.
chart—Placeholder.

10

Comments, end to end

Before, there was no way to write a comment; the first review said “comments not built / general.comment”. Now a comments capability with three procedures — start a thread, reply, resolve or reopen — and a general.comment lens that any category can open on a thread. The author and project come from the request's scope; a thread in another project is not found rather than refused. Reading is not part of the capability: threads and comments are rows, and the panels read them through store.read like any table. Page 09 covers the capability and the lint rules it had to satisfy.

10.1 · The capability

10.2 · The comment lens

State chip in the actions; author as a PanelActor; the comment as a quote; Reply and Resolve directly under it; an Anchored to row that opens the resource on the right slide and the object's threads; replies oldest first; a composer. The deck runtime is obtained in an effect rather than a derived, because attaching a runtime inside a derived was a state mutation during derivation.

"Someone";++  const first = $derived(comments[0]);+  const replies = $derived(comments.slice(1));+  const resolved = $derived(thread?.resolution !== undefined);++  const resourceTitle = $derived.by(() => {+    if (thread === undefined) return undefined;+    const table = TABLE[thread.target.kind];+    if (table === undefined) return undefined;+    const answer = read({ path: `${table}.${thread.target.id}.title` });+    const found = answer.current;+    return found?.kind === "field" && typeof found.value === "string" ? found.value : undefined;+  });++  let deckRuntime = $state<ReturnType<typeof view.slideDeckRuntime> | undefined>(undefined);+  $effect(() => {+    const held = thread;+    deckRuntime = held?.target.kind === "slides" ? view.slideDeckRuntime(held.target.id) : undefined;+  });+  const deckBody = $derived(deckRuntime?.body);++  const holding = (elements: Element[], elementId: string): Element | undefined => {+    for (const element of elements) {+      if (element.id === elementId) return element;+      const inner = element.content.children === undefined ? undefined : holding(element.content.children, elementId);+      if (inner) return inner;+    }+    return undefined;+  };++  const anchor = $derived.by((): { label: string; slideId?: string; elementId?: string } => {+    const within = thread?.within;+    if (within === undefined) return { label: "The whole thing" };+    if (within.kind === "text") return { label: "A passage" };+    if (within.kind === "cell") return { label: "A cell" };+    const slides = (deckBody?.slides ?? []) as { id: string; elements: Element[] }[];+    if (within.kind === "slide") {+      const at = slides.findIndex((slide) => slide.id === within.slideId);+      return { label: at === -1 ? "A slide" : `Slide ${at + 1}`, slideId: within.slideId };+    }+    if (within.kind === "element" && within.elementId !== undefined) {+      const at = slides.findIndex((slide) => holding(slide.elements, within.elementId!) !== undefined);+      const element = at === -1 ? undefined : holding(slides[at].elements, within.elementId);+      const kind = element === undefined ? "Object" : (KIND[element.content.type] ?? "Object");+      return { label: at === -1 ? kind : `Slide ${at + 1} · ${kind}`, slideId: slides[at]?.id, elementId: within.elementId };+    }+    return { label: "Somewhere in it" };+  });++  const openAnchor = () => {+    if (thread === undefined) return;+    const category = CATEGORY[thread.target.kind];+    if (category === undefined) return;+    view.open({ category, resourceId: thread.target.id, focus: anchor.slideId });+    if (category !== "slide-deck-editor") return;+    if (anchor.elementId !== undefined) view.inspect("slide-deck-editor.threads", { kind: "threads", id: anchor.elementId });+    else if (anchor.slideId !== undefined) view.inspect("slide-deck-editor.threads", { kind: "threads", id: anchor.slideId });+  };++  const when = (at: number): string =>+    new Date(at).toLocaleString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short", hour: "2-digit", minute: "2-digit" });++  let draft = $state("");+  let sending = $state(false);+  let box = $state<HTMLTextAreaElement | null>(null);++  const refresh = () => Promise.all([threadRows.refresh(), commentRows.refresh()]);++  const send = async () => {+    const text = draft.trim();+    if (threadId === undefined || text === "" || sending) return;+    sending = true;+    try {+      await replyTo({ threadId, text });+      draft = "";+      await refresh();+    } finally {+      sending = false;+    }+  };++  const replyPressed = () => {+    if (draft.trim() === "") box?.focus();+    else void send();+  };++  const settle = async () => {+    if (threadId === undefined || sending) return;+    sending = true;+    try {+      await resolveThread({ threadId, resolved: !resolved });+      await refresh();+    } finally {+      sending = false;+    }+  };+</script>++<Panel title="Comment">+  {#snippet actions()}+    {#if thread}+      {#if resolved}+        <PanelChip tone="success">Resolved by {nameOf(thread.resolution?.by)}</PanelChip>+      {:else}+        <PanelChip tone="attention">Open</PanelChip>+      {/if}+    {/if}+  {/snippet}++  {#if thread}+    <div class="flex flex-col gap-2 pb-2">+      <div class="px-3">+        <PanelActor+          name={nameOf(first?.author.userId ?? thread.createdBy.userId)}+          role={when(thread._creationTime)}+          onselect={() => view.inspect("general.person", { kind: "person", id: first?.author.userId ?? thread.createdBy.userId ?? "" })}+        />+      </div>+      <PanelQuote>{first?.blocks[0]?.display ?? "(no text)"}</PanelQuote>+      <div class="flex flex-wrap gap-1 px-3">+        <PanelButton label="Reply" icon={CornerDownLeft} tone="primary" disabled={sending} onclick={replyPressed} />+        <PanelButton label={resolved ? "Reopen" : "Resolve"} icon={resolved ? RotateCcw : Check} disabled={sending} onclick={() => void settle()} />+      </div>+    </div>++    <h3 class="band">Anchored to</h3>+    <PanelRow title={resourceTitle ?? "Resource"} sub={anchor.label} onselect={openAnchor} />++    <h3 class="band">Replies<span class="count">{replies.length}</span></h3>+    {#each replies as comment (comment._id)}+      <div class="flex flex-col gap-1 py-1">+        <div class="px-3">+          <PanelActor name={nameOf(comment.author.userId)} role={when(comment._creationTime)} onselect={() => view.inspect("general.person", { kind: "person", id: comment.author.userId ?? "" })} />+        </div>+        <PanelQuote>{comment.blocks[0]?.display ?? ""}</PanelQuote>+      </div>+    {/each}+    <div class="flex flex-col gap-1.5 px-3 pt-1">+      <Textarea+        bind:ref={box}+        bind:value={draft}+        placeholder="Reply…"+        class="text-body-sm field-sizing-content min-h-14 resize-none"+        onkeydown={(event: KeyboardEvent) => {+          if (event.key === "Enter" && (event.metaKey || event.ctrlKey)) {+            event.preventDefault();+            void send();+          }+        }}+      />+      <div class="flex justify-end">+        <Button size="xs" disabled={sending || draft.trim() === ""} onclick={() => void send()}><Send aria-hidden="true" />Send</Button>+      </div>+    </div>+  {:else}+    <PanelEmpty title="This thread is not here any more." />+  {/if}+</Panel>++<style>+  .band {+    display: flex;+    align-items: baseline;+    gap: calc(var(--token-spacing-unit) * 1.5);+    margin: 0;+    padding: calc(var(--token-spacing-unit) * 1.5) calc(var(--token-spacing-unit) * 3);+    font-size: var(--token-text-caption);+    line-height: var(--token-text-caption-leading);+    font-weight: 600;+    letter-spacing: 0.04em;+    text-transform: uppercase;+    color: var(--token-ink-secondary);+  }++  .count {+    margin-inline-start: auto;+    font-weight: 400;+    letter-spacing: 0;+    text-transform: none;+    color: var(--token-ink-muted);+    font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;+  }+</style>
11

Built, then reversed during review

Things that existed at some point on this branch and were removed because a review asked; none of them is in the working tree, so none has a diff.

  • A textarea in the text-box lens for editing the text off-slide (“no textarea”). Its section, components/element-text.svelte, was deleted once nothing mounted it; typing.ts · diffed, which it used, stays because a unit test covers it.
  • A “header rows” count on the table lens. The block still has headerRows and the renderer still shades the first row; only the control went.
  • A Weight select in the Style panel, replaced by Font colour.
  • The caret-position copy on the next-letter lens, and the Text section on the shape lens.
  • “Slide n of m” in the strip, the character count under notes, “Edit on slide” on the text lens.
  • A layout picker on the slide lens: a slide keeps the layout it was made with.
  • The Stage panel and the Theme panel's Type / Colours / Usage sections.
  • Steppers on numbers, a swatch dropdown before the native picker, “Centre” for “Center”.
  • The Konva canvas.
12

What was verified, and how

  • Unit tests. pnpm vitest run in app/: 635 tests pass, including the rewritten applier tests and the new tests for deck, stage, inserting, tables, arrange and comments.
  • Types. pnpm exec svelte-check: no errors in any file on this page. The errors it still reports are in new-tab/content/launcher.svelte and in categories whose $capabilities/* modules are intentionally not built; both predate the branch.
  • Structure. node scripts/lint.mjs: 34 findings, the same 34 as at the branch point. The rules this work had to satisfy are listed on page 09.
  • In the browser. Playwright scripts against the dev server on port 3001 opened the seeded deck and drove: typing into a shape, a text box growing, the native picker, posting a comment and seeing the badge, drag-selecting cells and merging, click-to-place, right-click without selecting, marquee from the gutter, group click and double-click, per-block font size, save-as-layout then New ▾ from it, the notes lens, shadow directions, the cell lens. Those scripts live in the session's scratchpad, not in the repository.
